    "content": "You are a board-certified psychiatrist interviewing a patient to reach a diagnosis.\nYour goal is to reach the correct diagnosis within 20 turns of talking with the patient. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n15\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF30.2, F31.2\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hi, it’s good to meet you. What brings you in today?\n\nPatient: I’m here because… it’s the voices, mainly. I know how that sounds, but I keep hearing them at work, like coming through the call-light speaker or the hallway vents. It’s not muffled like normal building noise—it’s clear, like somebody is standing right next to me and telling me which patient records have been changed.\n\nAnd it happens even when the unit is quiet. Like when there’s no one out in the hall, no alarms going off, nobody talking at the desk. I’ll hear it, and I’ll look around, and nobody else seems to hear anything. It’s been really upsetting, because it feels so real in the moment, and I can’t just ignore it when it’s about patient records.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been hearing these voices?\n\nPatient: It’s been about two months now, I think. It started around when they asked me to help lead the wound-care documentation rollout at work. At first I thought it was just stress or people talking nearby, because there was so much going on with the records and training people, but it kept happening. So… about two months, since that whole rollout started.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe someone is actually using the speaker or vents to send you these messages?\n\nPatient: I mean… yes, I think there’s something real happening. I don’t know exactly who is doing it or how, and I know that’s the part that sounds strange, but I can’t just accept that it’s nothing.\n\nThe wound-care charts have a pattern. I’ve been looking at them closely because that’s part of what they had me doing, and there are changes that don’t make sense—entries shifted, things coded a certain way, times that don’t line up. My supervisor keeps saying it’s just late entries or coding mistakes, like people are busy and they fix things later, but it doesn’t feel random to me. It’s too consistent.\n\nAnd then when I write something down or flag it, I get this sense that management is watching what I’m putting in the chart, like they know I’m the one who noticed it. I’m not trying to cause trouble, I’m really not, but I caught something they don’t want people to see. So when I hear the messages through the speakers or the vents, it feels connected to that—like it’s about the records and about me noticing the pattern.\n\nDoctor: During these past two months, have you had a period where you felt unusually energized and needed much less sleep than usual?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, that’s been happening. I don’t know if I would have called it “energized” at first, because I kept telling myself I was just trying to stay on top of everything, but it’s more than normal tired-but-busy.\n\nI’ll work a full shift, be on my feet all day, and then I get home and instead of crashing like I used to, I’ll start cleaning the kitchen, wiping down counters, reorganizing things, rewriting my notes from work so they’re clearer, sorting supplies or papers into piles. My husband will be sitting there saying, “Please just sit down for a minute,” and I’ll tell him I can’t yet because if I stop, I feel like I’ll lose track of something important.\n\nSometimes I just pace around the kitchen while I’m thinking, back and forth, back and forth, and he gets frustrated because it’s late and he wants me to come to bed. But I don’t feel ready to sleep. My body feels like it still has a motor running, even after the whole day.\n\nDoctor: During this time, have you felt like you had special abilities or an unusually important role?\n\nPatient: Yes… I guess I have. I don’t mean like, “special abilities” like a superhero or something. It’s not that. It’s more like… I feel like I’m the one who can see what’s actually happening in the wound-care charts when everyone else is brushing it off.\n\nBecause the patterns are there. I know they are. The entries, the times, the way things are coded—it’s not just random mistakes. My supervisor keeps saying, “Oh, those are late entries,” or “That’s just someone fixing documentation,” but I’ve worked with these records enough to know when something doesn’t fit. And it keeps lining up in a way that feels deliberate.\n\nSo yes, I do feel like I have an important role in it, because I caught it. I noticed what management doesn’t want anyone to see. And now when I write things down or flag something, I can tell they’re watching what I put in. It feels like they know I’m the one who saw the pattern, and that makes it hard to just pretend it’s nothing.\n\nDoctor: During this time, have you felt unusually happy, euphoric, or especially irritable?\n\nPatient: Yes… I mean, not happy exactly, like not carefree happy. It’s more like some days I feel suddenly clear, like I’m finally awake and everybody else is moving too slowly. Like I can see what needs to be fixed at work, and I’m supposed to be the one to fix it or at least point it out.\n\nAnd when I’m in that state, I can get really impatient. If my husband tells me to slow down or sit down, I snap at him. I don’t mean to, but it comes out sharp, like he’s trying to stop me from doing something important. And if a nurse at work tells me I’m overthinking it or says, “Just leave it alone for now,” I get irritated fast, because it feels like they’re blocking me or dismissing something that matters.\n\nSo yes, there are days where I feel… almost charged up, like I have a purpose. But it can turn into anger really quickly if someone gets in my way.\n\nDoctor: Have the voices ever continued during a time when your mood and energy felt back to your usual self?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think so. Not when I’m really back to myself.\n\nThe call-light voices and the messages in the charts, that all started after I was already sleeping less and running around like I couldn’t shut off. I was working nonstop, staying up, going over things again and again, feeling like I had to catch every mistake and fix what was happening at the facility. It wasn’t something I’d ever dealt with before, like years ago or during normal weeks.\n\nBefore this whole period where I got so driven and wound up about the rollout, I didn’t hear voices from the call lights or feel like the chart entries had hidden messages for me. It came after I was already… revved up, I guess. Like my mind was locked onto it and I couldn’t let it go.\n\nDoctor: Have there been at least 7 days in a row when you felt this wound-up and needed much less sleep?\n\nPatient: Yes. Definitely more than seven days. It’s been… I guess about two months now, since they asked me to help lead the wound-care documentation rollout.\n\nAt first I thought it was just because the rollout was stressful, you know, like extra responsibility and everyone needing answers, but it didn’t stop after a few days. It just kept going. Week after week I’d feel wound up, like I had to stay on top of it, and sleep felt like something I could put off because there was too much to do or too much to look over.\n\nSo yes, it hasn’t been just one bad week. It’s been going on for around two months.\n\nDoctor: Have you been hospitalized because of this episode?\n\nPatient: I… I’m not sure how to answer that exactly. I mean, I haven’t really thought of it in those terms.\n\nDo you mean admitted, like kept overnight on a unit? Because I don’t think I can say that’s happened from what I know. People have been worried, and there’s been a lot of talk around me about whether I’m okay and whether this has gotten out of hand, but as far as actually being hospitalized… I don’t have a clear yes to give you.\n\nI’m sorry, I know that sounds evasive. I’m not trying to be. I just don’t want to say something wrong.\n\nDoctor: Before this two-month period, have you ever had another separate episode in your life when you felt this same kind of unusually high energy and needed much less sleep?\n\nPatient: I don’t really have a clear example of that, no. I’m trying to think back, because I don’t want to just say “never” if I’m forgetting something, but nothing stands out to me like this.\n\nI mean, I’ve had busy stretches in my life, especially with work and responsibilities, where I was tired and pushing through or maybe sleeping less for a few nights because there was a lot going on. But this specific kind of feeling—where it’s like my energy is way up and I don’t seem to need the sleep the way I normally would—I can’t point to another separate time before these last couple months.\n\nSo… as far as I know, no. Not like this.\n\nDoctor: Before this two-month period, have you ever had a time in your life when you felt depressed nearly every day for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I can give you a clean yes or no on that. I mean… I’ve had times where I was sad, or worn down, or just felt kind of heavy from life and work and everything, but when you say depressed nearly every day for at least two weeks, I don’t know that I can point to a specific period like that before this.\n\nI’d have to think hard about it, because sometimes when you’re in the middle of things you don’t label it that way, you just keep going, especially in my kind of work. But nothing is coming to mind where I’d say, “Yes, that was definitely two weeks or more of feeling depressed almost every day.”\n\nSo… I don’t think so, or at least I’m not aware of one.\n\nDoctor: Before these past two months, had you ever had another period in your life when you heard voices or believed people were sending you hidden messages?\n\nPatient: No… not before this. Not that I can remember, and I think I would remember something like that because it’s been so unsettling.\n\nThe voices with the call lights, that feeling like I was hearing something more than just the normal noise at work, and the sense that there were messages hidden in the charts or the paperwork… that didn’t happen earlier in my life. It’s been… I guess about two months now, since they asked me to help lead the wound-care documentation rollout.\n\nAt first I thought it was just because the rollout was stressful, you know, like extra responsibility and everyone needing answers, but it didn’t stop after a few days. It just kept going. Week after week I’d feel wound up, like I had to stay on top of it, and sleep felt like something I could put off because there was too much to do or too much to look over.\n\nSo yes, it hasn’t been just one bad week. It’s been going on for around two months.\n\nDoctor: Have you been hospitalized because of this episode?\n\nPatient: I… I’m not sure how to answer that exactly. I mean, I haven’t really thought of it in those terms.\n\nDo you mean admitted, like kept overnight on a unit? Because I don’t think I can say that’s happened from what I know. People have been worried, and there’s been a lot of talk around me about whether I’m okay and whether this has gotten out of hand, but as far as actually being hospitalized… I don’t have a clear yes to give you.\n\nI’m sorry, I know that sounds evasive. I’m not trying to be. I just don’t want to say something wrong.\n\nDoctor: Before this two-month period, have you ever had another separate episode in your life when you felt this same kind of unusually high energy and needed much less sleep?\n\nPatient: I don’t really have a clear example of that, no. I’m trying to think back, because I don’t want to just say “never” if I’m forgetting something, but nothing stands out to me like this.\n\nI mean, I’ve had busy stretches in my life, especially with work and responsibilities, where I was tired and pushing through or maybe sleeping less for a few nights because there was a lot going on. But this specific kind of feeling—where it’s like my energy is way up and I don’t seem to need the sleep the way I normally would—I can’t point to another separate time before these last couple months.\n\nSo… as far as I know, no. Not like this.\n\nDoctor: Before this two-month period, have you ever had a time in your life when you felt depressed nearly every day for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I can give you a clean yes or no on that. I mean… I’ve had times where I was sad, or worn down, or just felt kind of heavy from life and work and everything, but when you say depressed nearly every day for at least two weeks, I don’t know that I can point to a specific period like that before this.\n\nI’d have to think hard about it, because sometimes when you’re in the middle of things you don’t label it that way, you just keep going, especially in my kind of work. But nothing is coming to mind where I’d say, “Yes, that was definitely two weeks or more of feeling depressed almost every day.”\n\nSo… I don’t think so, or at least I’m not aware of one.\n\nDoctor: Before these past two months, had you ever had another period in your life when you heard voices or believed people were sending you hidden messages?\n\nPatient: No… not before this. Not that I can remember, and I think I would remember something like that because it’s been so unsettling.\n\nThe voices with the call lights, that feeling like I was hearing something more than just the normal noise at work, and the sense that there were messages hidden in the charts or the paperwork… that didn’t happen earlier in my life. I felt like I had to stay on top of every detail, like if I didn’t fix everything at the facility then something terrible would happen or everything would fall apart.\n\nBefore that, I mean, I’ve been stressed at work plenty of times.
